Once upon a time there was a little boy named Ben. Ben loved to explore the world around him. He saw many amazing things, like beautiful vases that were on display in a store. One day, Ben was walking through the store when he came across a very special vase. When Ben saw it he was amazed! He said, “Wow, that is a really amazing vase! Can I buy it?” The shopkeeper smiled and said, “Of course you can. You can take it home and show all your friends how amazing it is!” So Ben took the vase home and he was so proud of it! He called his friends over and showed them the amazing vase. All his friends thought the vase was beautiful and couldn't believe how lucky Ben was. And that's how Ben found an amazing vase in the store!
Once upon a time, there was a reliable otter named Ollie. He lived in a river with his family. They all loved to play and swim together. One day, Ollie's mom said, "Ollie, hurry and get some fish for dinner!" Ollie swam fast to catch fish. He saw his friend, the duck. "Hi, Ollie!" said the duck. "Hi, duck!" said Ollie. "I need to hurry and catch fish for my family." While Ollie was catching fish, he found a big shiny stone. He thought, "This is not a fish, but it is so pretty!" Ollie took the shiny stone home to show his family. They all looked at the shiny stone and smiled. The shiny stone made everyone happy, and they forgot about the fish for dinner.
One day, a little boy named Tim went to the park. He saw a big tiger. The tiger was not mean, but very easy to play with. Tim and the tiger played all day. They had lots of fun. Then, something unexpected happened. The tiger started to shake. Tim was scared. He did not know what was going on. But then, the tiger turned into a nice dog. Tim was very surprised. Tim and the dog played together now. They were very happy. The dog was easy to play with too. At the end of the day, Tim went home with his new friend.
Once upon a time there was a friendly little boy called Bob. Bob loved to pick flowers and look for birds. One day he decided to go outside with his friends to pick some more flowers. He suddenly noticed something weird on the ground. It was a big, green thumb! It was so big, Bob had never seen one before. Bob curiously leaned in to take a better look. He told his friends: "look everyone, I picked up this big thumb! What do we do with it?" His friends were very excited. They told him to pick it up and take it home to show his family. So Bob carefully picked up the friendly thumb and carried it back home. When he arrived, Bob happily showed the thumb to his family. His dad was amazed and hugged Bob to show his appreciation. From that day on Bob always kept the big, friendly thumb with him as a reminder that special things can be found anywhere.
Once upon a time, in a small house, there lived a little girl named Lucy. Lucy loved the color orange. She had an orange dress, an orange ball, and even an orange cat. One day, Lucy met a new friend. This friend was not like other friends. It was a spirit. The spirit was very nice and liked to play with Lucy. One day, Lucy and the spirit were playing with her orange ball. They were having so much fun. Then, Lucy's mom called her for dinner. Lucy said to the spirit, "I have to go eat now. Will you play with me later?" The spirit nodded and smiled. At dinner, Lucy told her mom about the spirit. But her mom did not believe her. She said, "Spirits are not real, Lucy. You have a big imagination." Lucy felt sad that her mom did not believe her. After dinner, she went back to play with the spirit. They played with the orange ball and had lots of fun. Lucy knew that even if others ignore her friend, the spirit was real and they could play together.

The car had a battery inside that made it go. Tim said, "Sam, the battery is tight in the car. It will not fall out." Sam smiled and they kept playing. They raced the car around the park, laughing and having fun. But then, something unexpected happened. A big dog came and took the toy car in its mouth! Tim and Sam were scared, but the dog just wanted to play too. They all played together, and the dog was very gentle with the car. In the end, Tim, Sam, and the dog became good friends.
Once upon a time, there was a sailor named Tom. Tom had a big boat. He liked to sail on the sea. One day, Tom saw a little fish. The fish was sad. It was lost and wanted to go home. Tom said, "I can help you, little fish. You can fit in my boat, and I will take you home." The little fish was happy. It jumped into Tom's boat. They sailed together on the sea. The sea was safe and calm. Tom and the little fish talked and laughed. They became good friends. At last, they found the fish's home. The little fish said, "Thank you, Tom, for helping me." Tom smiled and waved goodbye. He sailed away, knowing he had a new friend.
Once upon a time, there was a smooth vase. It was very pretty. The vase lived in a small house with a girl named Lily and her mom. One day, Lily and her mom went outside to play. Before they left, her mom said, "Lily, please close the door." Lily closed the door and they played all day. When they came back, they saw the smooth vase on the floor. It was broken! Lily and her mom were very sad. They could not fix the pretty vase. The end.
Sara and Ben wanted to decorate a bowl for their mom. They found a big bowl in the kitchen and some paint and brushes. They took the bowl and the paint to the backyard and put them on a table. "Let's make the bowl pretty with colors," Sara said. "OK, I will paint a flower," Ben said. They started to paint the bowl with different colors. Sara painted a red heart and Ben painted a yellow flower. They were having fun. But then, it started to rain. The rain was wet and cold. It made the paint run and drip. The bowl looked messy and ugly. "Oh no, the rain ruined our bowl!" Sara cried. "Mom will not like it," Ben said. They ran inside the house with the bowl. They were sad and wet. They showed the bowl to their mom. They said they were sorry. But mom smiled and hugged them. She said she loved the bowl and them. "It's a beautiful bowl," she said. "You made it with love and creativity. The rain made it special. It's like a rainbow bowl." Sara and Ben felt happy. They gave mom a kiss and thanked her. They learned that sometimes, things can be good even when they seem bad.
Once upon a time, in a small town, there was a deaf boy named Tom. Tom could not hear, but he was very good at seeing and feeling things. He had many friends who loved to play with him. They would play games like tag and hide-and-seek. Tom was very fast and good at finding his friends. One day, the town bell would ring at noon. Everyone was excited to hear the bell, but Tom knew he could not hear it. He felt sad, but his friends had a plan. They decided to help Tom feel the bell ring too. They took Tom to the big bell and let him touch it while it rang. When the bell rang, Tom could feel the strong vibrations and he smiled. The moral of the story is that even if someone is different or has a problem, like Tom being deaf, we can still find ways to help them and make them happy. We should always be kind and caring to everyone, no matter how different they are.
Tom and Lily were twins who liked to play in the park. One day, they saw a large maze made of green bushes. They wanted to try it and find the way out. They ran inside the maze and followed the paths. Sometimes they turned left, sometimes they turned right. They saw many flowers and birds, but they did not see the exit. They started to feel lost and scared. They heard a loud roar behind them. It was a big lion who lived in the maze. He was hungry and angry. He wanted to catch Tom and Lily and eat them. Tom and Lily ran away from the lion. They looked for a place to hide. They saw a small hole in the bushes. They crawled inside and hoped the lion would not find them. The lion came closer and closer. He smelled Tom and Lily in the hole. He reached his paw inside and tried to grab them. He pinched Lily's leg with his sharp claws. Lily cried out in pain. Tom was brave and smart. He saw a stick on the ground. He picked it up and poked the lion's eye with it. The lion howled and backed away. He was hurt and afraid. Tom and Lily saw their chance. They ran out of the hole and out of the maze. They found their mom and dad waiting for them. They hugged them and told them what happened. They were happy and safe. They never went back to the maze again.
Once upon a time there was a person named Sue. She weighed a lot and her momma said that she had to start eating less and exercising more. So, Sue decided to start walking around the block every day. The first day Sue felt like it was too hard. But after a few days, Sue found it was easy! She started to feel lighter and lighter everyday. Soon Sue wanted to do something more fun for exercise. Sue's momma suggested that she try dancing to her favorite songs on the television. Sue thought this was a great idea. So, Sue watched the television, picked out her favorite songs and started to dance. It was hard at first but because it was fun, she soon found it was easy. And soon, Sue was weighing barely anything at all!

Tim's mom didn't like it, but Tim really needed to go. Tim went inside the toilet and tried to use it. But, suddenly, the toilet broke and water came out. Tim got very wet and dirty. He cried and said, "Mom, I don't like this toilet!" His mom hugged him and said, "I know, Tim. We will find a better one next time." They went home with Tim still feeling sad and wet.
One day, a little girl named Lucy went to the park with her mom. They saw a big tree with lots of flowers. The flowers had sweet smells. Lucy liked the smells. She wanted to count the flowers on the tree. "Mom, can I count the flowers?" Lucy asked. Her mom said, "Yes, you can count the flowers. Be careful not to touch them." Lucy started to count. "One, two, three," she said. But then, she saw something strange. There was some powder on the flowers. She touched the powder and it was soft. She showed her mom. "Look, mom! What is this powder?" Lucy asked. Her mom smiled and said, "That is flower powder. It helps make more flowers." Lucy was happy to learn something new. She finished counting the flowers and they went home. Now, Lucy knew that the sweet-smelling flowers had special powder to help them grow.

They were happy to be together. And they lived happily ever after.

The bird became Tim's new friend, and they played together in the park all day.

"Can we pet him?" Ben asks. "He looks nice." They walk to the guard and the dog. The guard smiles at them. "Hello, boys," he says. "This is Rex. He is a good dog. He likes children. Do you want to pet him?" "Yes, please!" Sam and Ben say. They reach out their hands. Rex wags his tail. He licks their faces. Sam and Ben giggle. They pet Rex for a while. They feel his soft fur. They gaze at his brown eyes. They see his sharp teeth. "Wow, he is so cool!" Sam says. "Thank you for letting us pet him," Ben says. "You're welcome," the guard says. "Rex likes you. You are very polite." Sam and Ben say goodbye to the guard and Rex. They run back to the slide. They are happy. They have a new friend.

Once upon a time, there was a pale lion who lived in a tunnel. The lion was not happy because he could not roar like the other lions. Every time he tried to roar, only a small sound came out. This made the lion very sad. One day, a little bird came to the tunnel. The bird told the lion that there was a magic fruit in the forest that could help him roar. The lion was excited and went to look for the fruit. He found it under a big tree and ate it right away. After eating the magic fruit, the lion felt a strange feeling in his throat. He tried to roar, and to his surprise, he roared so loud that it shook the whole forest. The other lions came running to see what happened. They saw the pale lion and were amazed by his big roar. The lions became friends and lived happily together, and the pale lion was never sad again.
Once upon a time, there was a big pumpkin. It was hot outside, and the pumpkin was sad. It wanted to be cool like the other pumpkins in the garden. The pumpkin saw a bird sitting on a tree branch and asked, "Can you teach me how to be cool like the other pumpkins?" The bird said, "I can teach you how to make a big shade. Then you will be cool like the other pumpkins." The pumpkin was happy to learn from the bird. So, they worked together to make a big shade using leaves and sticks. But then, a strong wind came and blew the shade away. The pumpkin was sad again. The bird told the pumpkin, "It's okay. Sometimes things don't work out the way we want them to. The important thing is to keep trying and never give up." The pumpkin learned to be strong and keep trying, even when things were hard. And soon, the weather changed, and the pumpkin was cool and happy like the other pumpkins. The moral of the story is to never give up and keep trying, even when things are tough.
Sara and Tom were playing in the garden. They liked to look for bugs and flowers. Sara had a mint plant in a pot. She liked to smell it and taste it. Tom wanted to try it too. "Can I have some mint?" he asked Sara. "Sure, here you go," Sara said. She gave him a leaf. Tom put it in his mouth. He made a face. It was very strong and spicy. "Yuck, I don't like it!" he said. He spat it out. Sara laughed. "It's good for you," she said. "It makes your breath fresh." Just then, they heard a loud noise. It was a dog barking. They looked up and saw a big black dog running towards them. It had sharp teeth and a long tongue. It looked angry. "Help, help!" Sara and Tom shouted. They were frightened. They dropped the mint and ran to the house. The dog chased them. "Go away, go away!" they cried. Their mom heard them and came out. She saw the dog and yelled at it. "Bad dog, bad dog! Leave my kids alone!" she said. She picked up a stick and waved it at the dog. The dog stopped and ran away. Sara and Tom hugged their mom. They were safe. "Thank you, mom," they said. "You're welcome, my loves," she said. "Are you okay?" "Yes, we're okay," they said. They told her about the mint and the dog. She smiled and kissed them. "I'm glad you're okay," she said. "But next time, be careful. Don't go near strange dogs. And don't eat too much mint. It might give you a tummy ache."

He has a big bag of letters and packages. He says, "Hello, I am the postman. I have some post for you." Lily opens the door and smiles. She says, "Hello, postman. Thank you for bringing me the post. You are very polite." Tom gives Lily the post. He says, "You are welcome, customer. I like to perform my job well. Do you have any post to send?" Lily thinks for a moment. She says, "Yes, I do. I have a card for my grandma. Can you take it to her?" Tom nods. He says, "Of course, I can. I will deliver it fast and safe. Where does your grandma live?" Lily says, "She lives in the yellow house on the hill. You can see it from here. It has a big garden and a swing." Tom says, "I know that house. It is very pretty. I will go there now. Goodbye, customer." Lily says, "Goodbye, postman. Have a nice day." Tom and Lily wave to each other. They are happy. They like to play pretend. They are good friends.
Once upon a time there was a fish who was very happy in the water. He swam around with his fish friends and spent all day exploring the deep blue ocean. One day, the fish did something very foolish. He swam too close to the surface and ate a fisherman’s bait. As soon as he did, the fisherman pulled him out of the water. The fish was so scared and he felt so sad. But the fisherman was very kind. He knew that the fish had made a mistake, so he put him back in the water and said “I forgive you”. The little fish was so grateful, and he learned an important lesson – never do something foolish again! He made sure he listened to the wise fish and stayed away from the surface. The moral of this story is to always be kind and to forgive those who make mistakes. It’s important to be patient and understanding with others, even when they do something foolish.

One day, a little girl named Amy found a baby bird in her yard. The baby bird could not fly. Amy wanted to help the baby bird, so she picked it up and took it to her mom. "Mom, can we help this baby bird?" Amy asked. Her mom shrugged and said, "We can try, but it might be hard." Amy and her mom took the baby bird inside and gave it some food and water. They made a warm bed for the baby bird to sleep in. The next day, Amy went to her friend Tom's house. She told Tom about the baby bird. Tom said, "I don't think you should help the baby bird. It's not your job." But Amy knew it was the right thing to do. She said, "I want to be honest and kind, so I will help the baby bird." Amy and her mom took care of the baby bird for many days. They helped it get strong and learn to fly. One day, the baby bird was ready to go back to the wild. Amy and her mom took the baby bird outside and said goodbye. The baby bird flew away, happy and free. Amy felt good because she had been honest and kind. The moral of the story is to always be honest and kind, even when it's hard.

Tim's mom heard them and came outside. She saw the hole in the ball and said, "Don't worry, Tim. I can fix it." Tim's mom went inside the house and came back with a patch. They played until the sun went down. They had a great day playing their favorite sport. And Tim learned that when something is terrible, it can be fixed with a little help from his mom.

Some were smooth and some were bumpy. Lily found a shell that was pink and shiny. She liked it very much. She showed it to Ben and said, "Look, Ben, I found a pretty shell. Do you want to see?" Ben nodded and took the shell from Lily. He held it to his ear and listened. He heard a soft sound, like the waves. Ben smiled and said, "Lily, this shell can tell us a story. It can tell us about the sea and the fish and the boats. It can tell us how peaceful it is under the water. Do you want to hear?" Lily nodded and took the shell from Ben. She held it to her ear and listened. She heard the same sound, like the waves. Lily and Ben took turns listening to the shell. They imagined they were swimming with the fish and riding the boats. They felt happy and calm. They thanked the shell for telling them a story. They put the shell in their bucket and went to look for more shells. They had a fun day at the beach.

Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tom. He lived in a small house with his mom and dad. One day, Tom's mom said, "Tom, do you remember the white landscape we saw last winter?" Tom nodded his head and smiled. Tom and his mom went outside to play in the snow. The ground was white and the trees were white too. They made a big snowman with a hat and a scarf. Tom said, "Look mom, our snowman is so big and white!" As they played, a friendly dog came to join them. The dog wagged its tail and barked, "Woof! Woof!" Tom laughed and said, "Hi doggy, do you like the white landscape too?" The dog barked again and ran around in the snow. Tom, his mom, and the dog played in the white landscape all day. He dreamt of the white landscape, the snowman, and the friendly dog. It was a day he would never forget.

Remy felt warm and happy. They played together all day. From that day on, Remy and Teddy always remembered the warm spot near the hedge.

Tim and Jen were twins who liked to play in the snow. They made a big snowman with a hat, a scarf and a carrot nose. They named him Bob and gave him a hug. "Bob is our friend," Tim said. "He will stay with us forever." "No, he won't," Jen said. "He will melt when the sun comes out. Then he will be gone." Tim did not like that. He wanted Bob to stay. He got angry at Jen. He threw a snowball at her. Jen threw one back. They started to fight. "Stop it, you two!" Mom said. She came out of the house. She saw Bob and smiled. "What a nice snowman you made. But you should not fight. Fighting is bad." "Jen said Bob will melt," Tim said. "I don't want him to melt. I want him to stay." "Tim, Jen is right," Mom said. "Bob will melt sooner or later. That's what happens to snow. But you can always make another one. And you can take a picture of Bob to remember him." Tim and Jen did not like that. They did not want another snowman. They wanted Bob. They did not want a picture. They wanted Bob. They cried and hugged Bob. But Mom was right. The next day, the sun came out. It was warm and bright. Bob started to melt. His hat fell off. His scarf slipped. His carrot nose dropped. His chubby body got smaller and smaller. Tim and Jen watched and cried. Bob was gone. They had no friend. They had no snowman. They had nothing. They were sad.
Once upon a time, in a deep, deep forest, there lived a rabbit and a bear. They were good friends and liked to play together. One day, they decided to play a game called "freeze." "Let's take turns," said the rabbit. "When I say 'freeze,' you stop moving. When you say 'freeze,' I stop moving." "Okay," said the bear. "That sounds fun!" They started to play. The rabbit hopped around and the bear stomped his big feet. The rabbit said, "Freeze!" The bear stopped moving. He stood very still. The rabbit laughed and hopped around him. Then, it was the bear's turn. He said, "Freeze!" The rabbit stopped hopping and stood very still. The bear laughed and stomped around the rabbit. After playing for a while, they sat down to rest. The rabbit said, "I like this game. What is your opinion?" The bear thought for a moment and said, "I like it too. It's fun to play with you." And so, the rabbit and the bear played "freeze" many more times in the deep, deep forest, always having lots of fun together.

Anna has pink sunglasses and Ben has blue sunglasses. One day, they decide to play pirates. They put on their sunglasses and pretend they are looking for treasure. Anna has a map and Ben has a shovel. They dig in the sandbox and find some shiny stones and shells. They are very happy and put them in a bucket. But then, Ben is clumsy and trips over the bucket. He falls and spills the treasure all over the ground. He also breaks his sunglasses. He starts to cry. Anna feels sorry for him and gives him a hug. She says they can share her sunglasses and find more treasure. Ben smiles and says thank you. They are good friends and continue to play pirates.
The sun shone brightly on a beautiful day, and Mr. and Mrs. Beaver were getting ready to go outside to have some fun. Mrs. Beaver turned to her husband, who had already put on his coat, and said, "Do you dare me to jump in the river?!" Mr. Beaver laughed, and said, "Of course!" Mrs. Beaver, who was feeling brave, ran to the edge of the river, then jumped in! She quickly surfaced, and said, "That wasn't so bad!" Mr. Beaver was so proud of Mrs. Beaver, that he said, "Let's split the pie and have a picnic!" Mrs. Beaver gave her husband a grateful smile, and Mr. Beaver grabbed a pie from his basket. He quickly split it into two pieces, and the two of them enjoyed their picnic in the sun. After they had finished, Mr. Beaver said, "I dare you to jump in the river again!" Mrs. Beaver laughed, and said, "I don't think I'm feeling that brave today!" But Mr. Beaver knew that she had conquered her fear, and he felt so proud and grateful of her bravery. So, the two of them enjoyed the rest of the day in the sunshine and laughed about the adventure.

John smiled and said, "You're welcome. See you soon!" Then he ran back to his home, happy that his mum will permit him much more fun the next day.

Lily was happy to learn about the tree. As they were kneeling, they felt the warm sun on their backs. Lily looked up and saw a bird in the tree. The bird said, "Tweet, tweet! Lily and Tom were playing in the bath. They had many toys: a duck, a boat, a frog and a ball. They made the toys swim and splash in the water. They had fun. But then Lily saw something. The water was going down. It was going into a hole. The hole was called a drain. Lily did not like the drain. She was scared of it. "Tom, look!" Lily said. "The water is becoming less. The drain is taking it away. What if it takes us away too?" Tom looked at the drain. He was not scared of it. He was curious. He wanted to see how it worked. "Don't worry, Lily," Tom said. "The drain is not bad. It is just a little hole. It cannot take us away. We are too big. Only the water can go through it. See?" Tom put his hand near the drain. He felt the water pulling his hand. He laughed. "It tickles!" Tom said. "It is like a game. Do you want to try?" Lily shook her head. She did not want to try. She did not like the game. She wanted the water to stay. "Tom, stop!" Lily said. "I don't like the drain. I don't like the game. I want more water. Please, turn on the tap!" Tom saw that Lily was sad. He did not want to make her sad. He wanted to make her happy. He turned on the tap. The water came out. It filled the bath again. The toys floated and bobbed. Lily smiled. "Thank you, Tom," Lily said. "You are a good brother. I like the water. I like the toys. I like you." Tom smiled too. He was a good brother. He liked the water. He liked the toys. He liked Lily. They hugged. They played. They forgot about the drain. They were happy.

Sam and Ben were brothers who liked to play outside. One day, they saw a big mill near the river. The mill had a big wheel that turned with the water. Sam and Ben wanted to see the mill up close. "Let's go to the mill," Sam said. "Maybe we can find something fun inside." "No, Sam, that's a bad idea," Ben said. "The mill is old and dirty. It might be dangerous. Mom and Dad told us not to go near the mill." But Sam did not listen to Ben. He was curious and brave. He ran to the mill and opened the door. He saw a lot of wood and hay inside. He also saw a box of matches on a table. "Wow, look at this," Sam said. He picked up a match and struck it on the box. The match made a bright flame. Sam liked the flame. He threw the match on the hay. "Sam, what are you doing?" Ben shouted. "You are stupid! You will burn the mill!" But Sam did not hear Ben. He struck another match and threw it on the wood. The wood and the hay caught fire. The fire grew bigger and bigger. The mill was burning. "Help, help!" Sam and Ben cried. They ran out of the mill. They saw smoke and flames coming out of the mill. They were scared and sorry. A man who worked at the mill saw the fire. He called the firemen. The firemen came with a big truck and a hose. They sprayed water on the fire. They put out the fire. The man was angry. He asked Sam and Ben who started the fire. Sam and Ben told the truth. They said they were sorry. The man said they were lucky they did not get hurt. He said they were stupid and naughty. He said they had to pay for the damage. He said he would tell their mom and dad. Sam and Ben felt ashamed. They wished they had listened to Ben. They learned a lesson. They never played with matches again. They never went near the mill again.

Lily and Ben were bored. They wanted to play outside, but it was raining. They looked around the house for something fun to do. They saw a big box in the corner. It had a sign that said "Do not open". "What is in there?" Lily asked. "I don't know. Let's find out," Ben said. They opened the box and saw many smaller boxes inside. They had holes and flaps and strings. They looked like a maze. "Wow, this is cool. Let's make a maze with the boxes," Lily said. They took out the boxes and arranged them on the floor. They made a long and twisty maze with many turns and dead ends. They crawled inside and explored the maze. They had fun finding each other and hiding and making noises. They did not hear their mom come home. She walked into the living room and saw the mess. She was grumpy. "What are you doing? Look at this mess. You are not supposed to open that box. It is for the charity sale," she said. Lily and Ben came out of the maze. They looked at their mom. They were sorry. "We are sorry, mom. We were bored. We wanted to play. We discovered a maze in the box. It was fun," Lily said. Ben nodded. He said, "We are sorry, mom. We did not know. We will help you clean up." Their mom sighed. She was not so grumpy anymore. She saw that they were just curious and creative. She smiled. "It's okay, kids. I understand. But next time, please ask me before you open a box. And please be careful with the boxes. They are for a good cause. Now, let's clean up and then we can have a snack. How about that?" she said. Lily and Ben smiled. They said, "Okay, mom. Thank you, mom. We love you, mom." They hugged their mom and helped her clean up the maze. They learned a lesson and had a snack. They were happy.

Tom and Lily liked to watch the water change colors and make shapes. One day, they went to the park with their mom. But when they got there, they saw that the fountain was not working. It was still and gray, and there was no water at all. Tom and Lily were sad. They asked their mom why the fountain was broken. Their mom said that the fountain needed water and power to work, and that sometimes they ran out. She said that the people who took care of the park would provide water and power soon, and then the fountain would work again. Tom and Lily did not understand what water and power were, but they trusted their mom. They decided to play with other things in the park while they waited for the fountain to come back. They ran, swung, slid and climbed. They had fun, but they still missed the fountain. They kept looking at it, hoping to see some water. After a while, they heard a sound. It was a loud and happy sound, like a splash. They turned around and saw that the fountain was working again. It was not gray anymore, it was bright and colorful. It made water go up and down in the air, and it changed colors and made shapes. Tom and Lily were happy. They ran to the fountain and watched it with joy. They thanked their mom for bringing them to the park, and they thanked the people who provided water and power for the fountain. They said that the fountain was their favorite thing in the park, and that they loved it very much.

One day, Ben and Lily go to the farm with their mom. They run to the barn to see the animals. They see cows, sheep, pigs, and chickens. They hear a loud neigh from the horse stall. They peek inside and see a big black horse with a white star on its forehead. "Wow, look at the horse!" Ben says. "He is so dark and shiny!" Lily says. They want to pet the horse, but they are a little scared. They see their grandpa coming with his leather hat and a bucket of carrots. "Hello, kids! Do you want to meet Star?" he says. "Who is Star?" Ben asks. "Star is my horse. He is very friendly and smart. He likes carrots. Do you want to feed him?" he says. "Yes, please!" Lily says. Grandpa gives them each a carrot and shows them how to hold their hands flat. He tells them to be gentle and calm. He leads them to the horse stall and lets them feed Star. Star munches the carrots and nuzzles their hands. He makes a soft snort and shakes his mane. Ben and Lily giggle and stroke his nose. They feel his warm breath and his soft fur. They think he is the best horse ever. "Can we ride him, grandpa?" Ben asks. "Maybe another day, when you are bigger. For now, you can just say hello and goodbye. He likes that too," he says. "Okay, grandpa. Thank you for letting us feed Star. He is so nice," Lily says. "You're welcome, kids. I'm glad you like him. He likes you too. Come on, let's go see the other animals. Maybe we can find some eggs in the chicken coop," he says. He puts his leather hat on his head and takes their hands. They walk out of the barn and into the sunny field. They are happy to visit their grandpa and his horse. They love the farm.

Tony answered, "Behind this sweater, I saw a scary bird. I think it has a nest in the park." Frank replied, "Let's check it out!" They peeked behind the sweater and saw a big, angry bird. Without warning, it began to scatter feathers everywhere. Tony and Frank ran away from the park, terrified. None of the fun games were able to cheer them up. Frank was still feeling tired, but this time it was because of the scary bird.
One day, a boy named Tom found a clean, shiny rock. He thought it was special and had value. He showed it to his friend, Sally. "Look what I found!" Tom said. "It's clean and shiny. I think it has value." Sally looked at the rock and said, "It's nice, but I don't think it has value. You should leave it here." Tom felt sad but agreed to leave the rock. They went to play in the park. While they were playing, a man saw the shiny rock. He picked it up and smiled. He knew it was special and had value. The man took the rock to a store. The store owner was happy to see the shiny rock. He gave the man lots of money for it. The man was very happy. Tom and Sally didn't know they left something so special.
Anna liked to spend time with her angel. Her angel was a doll with wings and a halo. She got it from her grandma for her birthday. She named her angel Lily. One day, Anna and Lily went to the park with her mom. They saw a big slide, a swing, and a sandbox. Anna wanted to play with everything. She asked her mom, "Can I go on the slide, mom?" "Yes, you can, Anna. But don't forget Lily. She likes to play too," her mom said. Anna nodded and ran to the slide. She put Lily on her lap and slid down. She laughed and said, "Whee! That was fun, Lily!" Then she went to the swing. She pushed Lily gently and said, "Look, Lily, you can fly like a real angel!" Then she went to the sandbox. She made a castle with sand and sticks. She put Lily on top and said, "You are the queen of the castle, Lily!" Anna was having a lot of fun. But she did not see the unknown boy who came to the sandbox. He was bigger than Anna and had a mean face. He saw Lily and wanted to take her. He grabbed Lily from the castle and said, "This is mine now!" Anna was shocked and scared. She cried and said, "No, no, no! Give Lily back! She is my angel!" The boy laughed and said, "No, she is mine! She is a stupid doll!" He ran away with Lily. Anna ran after him. She shouted, "Mom, mom, help! He took Lily!" Her mom heard her and saw what happened. She ran after the boy too. She caught him and said, "Hey, you! Give Lily back to Anna! She is her angel!" The boy was scared of her mom. He dropped Lily and ran away. Her mom picked up Lily and gave her to Anna. She hugged Anna and said, "It's okay, Anna. You have Lily back. She is your angel." Anna hugged Lily and said, "Thank you, mom. Thank you for saving Lily. I love you and I love Lily." Her mom smiled and said, "I love you too, Anna. And I love Lily. She is a very special angel."

Max said, "Hold on to me, Lily. I will take you down." Lily held on tight to Max, and they went down the tree together. Lily was happy and thanked Max for helping her. From that day on, Max and Lily became the best of friends.

The white shark tried to fix the hole with its nose. But the boat broke more. The man fell into the water. The white shark was very sorry, but it was too late. The boat was gone and the man was sad.
Once upon a time, there was a dependable dog named Spot. Spot loved to play with his best friend, a little girl named Lucy. One day, they were playing in the yard when they found a big pear tree. Lucy said, "Look, Spot! A pear tree! Let's pick a pear!" They picked the biggest pear they could reach. The pear was very big and very yummy-looking. Spot and Lucy wanted to share the pear with their friends, so they went to find them. They found their friends, a cat named Whiskers and a bird named Tweetie, playing nearby. Lucy said, "We found a big pear! Let's all share it!" Whiskers and Tweetie were happy to share the pear. They all took turns taking bites of the pear, and they all agreed that it was the best pear they had ever tasted. As they were eating, a squirrel came up to them and said, "That pear belongs to me! I picked it and dropped it by accident." Lucy, Spot, Whiskers, and Tweetie felt bad for taking the squirrel's pear. They all said sorry to the squirrel. The squirrel said, "It's okay, we can all share the pear." So, they all sat together and finished eating the big, yummy pear. They all became friends and played together every day under the pear tree.

He wrapped his warm coat closer to his body and built a snowman with his dad. "Look daddy!" he shouted, pointing at the snowman. Dad smiled. "It looks great." Just then, Jason saw a small smoke coming out of a chimney of the nearest house. He said, "Let's hope someone will give us hot chocolate." Dad smiled again. "That would be nice wouldn't it?" Jason smiled and nodded. "Yes, please. I'm so weak from playing in the snow." But when they reached the house, nobody answered the door. Jason was disappointed. "Let's hope someone else will help us," said Dad. Just then, an old man came out of a nearby house. He said, "Why, hello there. Let's warm up with some hot chocolate!" Jason smiled. He was very happy. Now he was hopeful that he could still get his hot chocolate!

Your name is Julie!" The little girl smiled and said, "Thank you Joe for finding my name!" Joe smiled back, patted her on the head and said, "You're welcome".

One day, they wanted to design a big castle. They used all their blocks and worked hard. They made walls, doors, windows and a flag. They were proud of their castle and wanted to show it to their mom. But when they went to look for their mom, they saw a young boy in their room. He was their cousin, Tom. He had come to visit with his mom. He was bored and saw the blocks. He did not know that Ben and Lily had designed a castle. He thought they were just blocks. He knocked down the castle and threw the blocks around. He laughed and said, "This is fun!" Ben and Lily came back and saw what Tom had done. They were very sad and angry. They shouted, "Tom, you are bad! You broke our castle! We worked hard to design it! Go away!" Tom did not care. He said, "I don't care. They are just blocks. You can make another castle. I want to play with them. Give them to me!" He tried to grab the blocks from Ben and Lily. They did not want to share. They pushed him away. They started to fight. They hit, kicked and bit each other. They cried and screamed. Their mom heard the noise and came to see what was wrong. She saw the mess and the fight. She was very upset and disappointed. They did not want to design anything anymore. They had lost their fun and their castle. They had a bad day.
Once upon a time, there was a strong lion. He had a problem. He wanted to build a big tall house in his jungle, but he was stuck. He didn't know how to solve it. Then one day, he saw a little monkey walking by. He asked the monkey, "Can you help me solve my problem?" The monkey smiled. He said, "Yes, I have an idea. I can get my whip and swing it at the trees. Then the trees will fall and you'll have enough wood to build your house!" The lion liked the idea. He said, "Thank you little monkey! You are so smart!" The monkey smiled. He grabbed his whip and started swinging it. Soon, enough trees had fallen and the lion was able to build his strong tall house. He was very happy and thanked the little monkey again. The little monkey was happy too. He was glad he could help his strong lion friend solve his problem. The end!
Once upon a time, there was an owl who loved to write. Every day, he would fly around the forest and fill notebook after notebook with stories, jokes, poems, and more. One day, the owl gathered his notebooks and flew to the nearby town. He was so excited to show people his work! But when he landed, the owl was sad to find that no one wanted to look at it. Everyone was busy and in a hurry. The owl flew back to the forest and asked another owl, "Why don't they like my stories?" The other owl replied, "When you don't take the time to care about others, they won't take the time to care about you." The owl felt sorry and flew away. He kept writing in the forest, but never showed anyone else his work. The moral of the story is that if you don't take the time to care about others, they won't take the time to care about you.

I need gentle water to wash my hands." Lily's big brother, Tom, heard her. He came to help her. "I can make the water gentle for you," he said. Tom turned the other tap, and the water became gentle. But then, the sink started to fill up too much. The water was going to spill out of the sink! "Oh no!" said Lily. "The sink is too full! What do we do?" Tom thought for a moment. "We need to turn off the water," he said. They both reached for the taps and turned them off together. The water stopped, and the sink was safe. Lily and Tom smiled at each other. They had worked together to fix the problem. Now, they could both wash their hands with the gentle water in the sink.

The small tree was happy and asked, "How can you help me?" The bird said, "I will sit on your branch, and you can lean on me. Then you can touch the sunshine." The small tree leaned on the bird and touched the sunshine. The big tree saw this and was surprised. The big tree asked the bird, "Can you help me too?" The bird said, "I am available to help you, but you must be kind to the small tree." The big tree learned to be kind and share the sunshine with the small tree. They both enjoyed the sunshine together with the help of the bird. The moral of the story is to be kind and share with others, because we can all enjoy the good things together.

But Tim's mom said, "If we work together, we can lift the weight." So, Tim and his mom put all the toys in the box. They tried to lift the box together. It was still heavy, but they could carry it. The moral of the story is: When things are hard, don't be afraid to ask for help and work together with others.

"OK, but be careful. The fan is loud and fast," Ben says. They run to the fan and feel the wind on their faces. They laugh and shout. They see some ducks in the pond. The ducks swim away from the fan. "Hey, ducks, come here!" Mia calls. "We want to feed you." Ben has an idea. He jokes with Mia. He says, "Mia, do you know why the ducks are scared of the fan?" "No, why?" Mia asks. "Because the fan is a big duck-eater!" Ben says. He makes a scary face and a quack sound. Mia screams and runs away from the fan. She thinks Ben is telling the truth. She is afraid of the fan. Ben laughs and follows her. He says, "Mia, Mia, wait! I was joking. The fan is not a duck-eater. It is just a fan. I'm sorry. Don't be mad at me." Mia stops and looks at Ben. She sees that he is smiling. She realizes that he was joking. She is not afraid of the fan anymore. She is not mad at Ben. She says, "Ben, you are silly. You scared me. But you are also funny. I forgive you." They hug and go back to the fan. They play and joke with each other. They have fun. They are happy.

One day, a cat and a dog were playing in a park. They saw a big box with the word "reverse" on it. They did not know what it meant, but they wanted to find out. The cat said, "Let's open it!" The dog agreed, "Yes, let's see what's inside!" When they opened the box, they found a lot of powder. They looked at each other and wondered what to do with it. The cat had an idea, "Let's put some on us and see what happens!" The dog thought it was a fun idea, so they both put some powder on themselves. Suddenly, something unexpected happened! The cat became a dog, and the dog became a cat. They were both very surprised. The cat, who was now a dog, said, "Wow, we have switched places! This is so strange!" The dog, who was now a cat, agreed, "Yes, I don't feel fit in this small body!" They decided to put the powder on themselves again to change back. When they did, they were happy to be themselves again. They learned that the "reverse" powder could switch things around, and they promised to be more careful next time.
One day, a rabbit named Bob and a turtle named Tim were in the woods. They were talking about who was faster. Bob said, "I think I am faster than you, Tim!" Tim replied, "No, I think I am faster than you, Bob!" They decided to have a race to see who had more speed. Bob was very fast and Tim was very slow. But Tim had a brilliant idea. He asked his friend, the bird, to help him. The bird picked up Tim and flew high in the sky. As the race started, Bob ran as fast as he could. Tim and the bird were flying above him. When they reached the finish line, Tim and the bird landed just before Bob got there. Tim won the race! Bob was surprised and said, "Wow, Tim! You are faster than I thought!" Tim smiled and said, "Thank you, Bob! I had a brilliant plan and a great friend to help me." From that day on, Bob and Tim were best friends and they always helped each other.

Anna loved to bake cakes with her mom. She liked to mix the flour, the eggs, the sugar and the butter in a big bowl. She liked to pour the batter in a round pan and put it in the oven. She liked to smell the sweet aroma of the cake as it baked. But most of all, she liked to taste the cake when it was done. One day, Anna decided to bake a cake by herself. She wanted to surprise her mom and dad. She remembered the ingredients and the steps, but she did not measure them carefully. She put too much sugar, too little butter, and forgot the eggs. She also added some salt, pepper, and ketchup, because she thought they would make the cake more colorful and spicy. When the cake was ready, Anna took it out of the oven and put it on a plate. She was very proud of her work. She called her mom and dad to the kitchen and said, "Look, I made a cake for you! Do you want to taste it?" Her mom and dad smiled and thanked her for her effort. They each took a small bite of the cake and tried not to make a face. The cake was very sweet, very dry, and very weird. They did not like it at all, but they did not want to hurt Anna's feelings. They said, "Wow, Anna, this is a very...interesting cake. You are very creative and brave to try new things. But you know, sometimes it is better to follow a recipe and measure the ingredients. That way, the cake will be more delicious and more balanced. Do you want to learn how to bake a cake with us next time?" Anna nodded and said, "Yes, please. I want to learn how to bake a cake like you. I'm sorry, I did not mean to make a bad cake. I just wanted to make you happy." Her mom and dad hugged her and said, "We are happy, Anna, because you are our daughter and we love you. You are very intelligent and kind, and we are proud of you. But remember, not everything that looks good tastes good. And not everyone who tastes your cake will be as nice as us. Some people might be mean and judge you. So, be careful and listen to your mom and dad, okay?" Anna said, "Okay, mom and dad. I love you too. And I love cake. Can we bake a cake together now?" They said, "Of course, Anna. Let's bake a cake together. And let's taste it together too." The end.

The moral of this story is that we should learn from our mistakes. Even when we failed, we should not feel guilty and take away an important lesson.

Once upon a time, in a big forest, there was a smart deer. This deer could do many things that other animals could not do. The smart deer had a special gift: it could speak like people. One sunny day, the smart deer was walking in the forest. It saw a little girl who was lost and crying. The deer went to the girl and spoke to her. "Don't cry, little girl," said the deer. "I will help you find your way home." The smart deer and the little girl walked together through the forest. They saw pretty flowers, tall trees, and happy birds. Soon, they found the girl's home. The little girl was so happy and thanked the deer for its help. The smart deer smiled and went back to the forest. From that day on, the girl and the smart deer were the best of friends.
Once upon a time, there was a seal who lived in the sea. The seal liked to play with his friends, the fish. One day, the seal wanted to escape and see the original big world outside the sea. The seal swam and swam until he reached the land. There, he met a big bird. The seal said, "Hi bird, I escape from the sea to see the big world. Can you show me around?" The bird nodded and said, "Yes, I can show you the big world." As they went around, the seal became very tired. He missed his friends, the fish, and his home in the sea. The bird took the seal to a very high place. The seal was scared and wanted to go back to the sea. But he could not escape from the high place. The seal was sad and could not go back to his home.
One day, a happy ball named Bounce loved to jump and play. Bounce had many friends in the park. They all liked to play together. But there was one big ball named Rude who was not nice. Rude was a threat to others because he made them sad. One sunny day, Bounce and his friends were playing in the park. They were having so much fun. But then, Rude came and started to be mean. Bounce and his friends were scared and sad. They did not want to play with Rude. But something unexpected happened. A kind wind blew Rude away from the park. Bounce and his friends were happy again. They played and laughed all day. And from that day on, Rude never came back to the park. Bounce and his friends never had to worry about Rude again.
Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. Tim liked to play in the park. One day, he saw a big box. The box had a picture of a skeleton on it. Tim was not scared. He wanted to find out what was inside the box. Tim asked his friend, Sam, to help him open the box. Sam said, "Okay, let's pass the box to each other and see what happens." They passed the box back and forth. Then, they heard a sound. It was a rare sound, like a small bell ringing. They opened the box and found a toy skeleton inside. The toy skeleton had a little bell. When they passed the toy, it made the rare bell sound. Tim and Sam played with the toy skeleton all day. They were very happy they found the rare toy in the park.
Once upon a time, there was a prince who loved to play. He wore a blue cape and loved to swing around in it while playing. One day, a mean dragon came and wanted to turn him into a puppet. The prince asked the dragon, "What do you want me to do?" The dragon laughed and said, "I'm going to turn you into a puppet and I'll pull the strings." The prince was very scared but he was determined not to let the dragon win. He tried to run away, but the dragon was too fast. He tried to fight back, but the dragon was too strong. Just when it seemed like the prince was going to give up, a magical fairy appeared. She told the prince, "Be brave and your courage will help you." She gave him a wand and said, "Use this and you will be safe!" The prince was so thankful and bravely took the wand. With one wave of the wand, a colorful rainbow appeared in the sky. "Follow the rainbow to safety," said the fairy. The prince did as he was told and followed the rainbow, away from the mean dragon. He was safe at last and felt very playful. He spun around in his cape and laughed with joy. The prince had learned an important lesson, that courage can help you defeat your enemies.
Once upon a time, in a small house, there lived a little girl named Amy. Amy had a big box of junk. She loved to play with her junk every day. In the box, there were old toys, pretty rocks, and colorful papers. One sunny day, Amy found a small toy that did not belong in her junk box. It was a tiny, cute cat. Amy wanted to find where the cat belonged. She looked around her house to find a safe place for the cat. Amy saw a soft, warm bed near the window. She thought, "This is a safe place for the cat." She put the cat on the bed and smiled. The cat looked happy, too. Now the cat belonged in the safe, warm bed, and Amy went back to play with her junk box.

It was so much fun! He acted with his pole all day. He waved it around and pretended he was catching something. He jumped around with it and pretended he was balancing on top of it. He was so imaginative. Soon, it was time to go inside. The young boy was sad. He had to put his pole away. But he knew he would have lots more time to act with it in the future. He hugged his pole and went inside a little happier.

Tim was very patient. He waited for the ball to come near him. When the ball got close, Tim gave it a big push. The ball rolled fast and far away. Tim and the red ball played together all day. They had so much fun. They laughed and pushed each other. At the end of the day, Tim and the red ball were very happy. They knew they would play together again soon.
Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. Tim was very polite. He loved to spin around and play with his toys. One day, Tim found a big box. It was a present. Tim was very happy. Tim wanted to open the present. He spun around and around, then he stopped. The present was gone! Tim was sad. He looked everywhere for the present. He asked his mom, but she did not know where it was. Then, Tim saw his dog, Max. Max had the present in his mouth. Tim was happy again. He took the present from Max and opened it. Inside the box was a toy that could spin too! Tim and Max played together and had lots of fun.
Sam and Lily wanted to go to the park. They had a map that showed them the way. But they did not know how to read the map. They got lost. They saw a big dog. The dog was bad. It barked and chased them. Sam and Lily ran away. They were scared. They found a house. They knocked on the door. A man opened it. He looked angry. "Who are you? What do you want?" he asked. "Please help us. We are lost. We want to go to the park," Sam said. The man did not help them. He grabbed the map from Sam's hand. He tore it up. He threw it away. "This is my map. You stole it. Go away. You are bad," he said. He slammed the door. Sam and Lily cried. They had no map. They had no park. They had no help. They did not know what to do. They did not settle. They were sad. The end.
Sara loves fashion. She likes to dress up in pretty clothes and shoes. She has many dolls that she also dresses up. She likes to show her dolls to her friends. One day, Sara gets a big box in the mail. It is from her aunt, who lives far away. Sara is very happy. She opens the box and sees many new clothes and shoes for her dolls. They are very beautiful and colorful. Sara wants to try them on her dolls right away. She runs to her room and takes out her dolls. She picks one doll and puts on a pink dress and a purple hat. She thinks the doll looks very nice. She picks another doll and puts on a blue skirt and a yellow shirt. She thinks the doll looks very cute. She wants to show her new fashion to her mom. She carries her dolls to the living room, where her mom is reading a book. She says, "Mom, look at my dolls! Auntie sent me new clothes and shoes for them. They are very pretty, right?" Her mom looks up and smiles. She says, "Yes, they are very pretty, Sara. Auntie is very nice to send you such a nice gift. You are very lucky." Sara is very happy. She hugs her mom and says, "Thank you, mom. I love you. And I love auntie too." She wants to show her new fashion to her dad. She carries her dolls to the kitchen, where her dad is making a sandwich. She says, "Dad, look at my dolls! Auntie sent me new clothes and shoes for them. They are very cute, right?" Her dad looks up and frowns. He says, "Sara, what are you doing? You are making a mess. You dropped some clothes and shoes on the floor. And you are getting crumbs on your dolls. Go back to your room and clean up." Sara is very sad. She feels angry. She says, "But dad, I just wanted to show you my new fashion. Auntie sent me a nice gift. You are not nice. You are mean." She runs back to her room, crying. She hugs her dolls and says, "Don't worry, dolls. I still love you. And auntie loves you too. And mom loves you too. But dad is angry. He doesn't like fashion. He is silly."
Once there was a princess who was looking for a prince to marry. She tried many colors to find the right one, but none seemed right. One day, she walked in the woods and saw a beautiful prince. He was wearing all kinds of colors and he looked very successful. She asked the prince to marry her and he said yes. The princess and the prince got married and they were very happy together. Everyone was impressed with the successful prince and they celebrated the marriage with many colors and gifts. The princess and the prince lived very happily ever after.
Once upon a time, there was a kind girl named Jane. She had the patience of an elephant, and was always happy to help those in need. One day, her mother asked her to iron her dress. Jane had never ironed a dress before, so she was a bit nervous. She asked her mother for help. Her mother told her very patiently, "Just remember, the iron needs to shine." With a deep breath, Jane started ironing her mother's dress. She was very careful, gently moving the iron on the dress, and letting it shine. She was proud of her work, and even more proud when her mother praised her. The next day, Jane tackled a bigger challenge - her father's shirt. It was a little tricky, but she remembered her mother's words and patiently ironed it until it shone. She showed it to her father, who gave her an extra hug and said, "Well done, Jane!" From then on, Jane was known in her family as the "iron queen."
Once upon a time, there was a little girl named Lily. She loved tea very much. Every day, she would have tea with her mom and dad. One day, Lily was having tea with her mom. She saw that her tea was spoiled. Lily started to cry. Her mom did not know what to do. Just then, a magic cat came into the room. The cat saw Lily cry and wanted to help. The cat waved its tail and the spoiled tea turned into yummy tea. Lily stopped crying and smiled. The magic cat had saved the day. From that day on, Lily and the magic cat had tea together, and they were very happy.
Lily and Ben are friends. They like to play in the park. They have a ball, a bike and a bell. The bell is on the bike. Lily likes to ring the bell. Ben likes to ride the bike. One day, they go to the park. Lily says, "Can I ring the bell?" Ben says, "Yes, you can. But be careful. Do not ring it too loud. Some people do not like loud noises." Lily nods. She takes the bell and rings it softly. It makes a nice sound. Ben gets on the bike. He pedals fast. He feels the wind on his face. He smiles. He sees a big hill. He wants to go down the hill. He thinks it will be fun. But he is careless. He does not look where he is going. He does not see a dog. The dog is sleeping on the grass. Ben rides the bike over the dog. The dog wakes up. The dog is angry. It barks and chases Ben. Lily hears the dog. She sees Ben. She is scared. She rings the bell loud. She hopes the dog will stop. But the dog does not stop. It runs faster. Ben hears the bell. He looks back. He sees the dog. He is scared too. He tries to pedal faster. But he loses his balance. He falls off the bike. He hurts his knee. He cries. Lily runs to Ben. She drops the bell. She hugs Ben. She says, "I am sorry. I wanted to help you. Are you okay?" Ben says, "I am okay. But my knee hurts. And the bike is broken. And the dog is still here." They look at the dog. The dog is not angry anymore. It is wagging its tail. It licks Ben's knee. It wants to play. Lily and Ben learn a lesson. They learn to be careful. They learn to look where they are going. They learn to respect the dog. They also learn to share. They give the dog the ball. The dog is happy. They are happy too. They are still friends.
One day, a cool cat named Tom found a shiny medal in his yard. He wanted to show it to his best friend, a dog named Max. Tom ran to Max's house with the medal. Max was so happy to see the shiny medal. Tom said, "Look, Max! I found a cool medal!" Max wanted to wear the medal. Max said, "Please, Tom, can I wear the medal?" Tom said, "Yes, Max, you can wear the medal." Max put the medal on and felt very proud. They played in the yard, wearing the cool medal. Suddenly, a big bird came and took the medal from Max's neck. Max and Tom were very sad. They asked the bird, "Please, give the medal back." The bird said, "I will give the medal back if you play a game with me." Tom and Max played the game with the bird, and they won! The bird gave the cool medal back to them. They were so happy and played together all day long.
Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. He lived in a small house with his mom, dad, and dog, Spot. Tim loved to play with Spot all day long. They would run, jump, and pass a red ball to each other. One day, Tim and Spot played near a big tree. Tim threw the ball very hard. The ball went up, up, up into the tree. "Oh no!" said Tim. Spot barked and tried to jump high, but he could not reach the ball. Tim had an idea. He ran back to the house and got a long stick. With the stick, he poked the ball in the tree. The ball fell down, and Spot caught it. Tim and Spot were so happy. They passed the ball to each other all day long. And the ball never got stuck in the tree again.

The elderly pilot got in his plane and flew up into the sky. He flew high and low, and he was very happy. He did not slip, and he had a great day. The end.
Once upon a time, there was a humble dog named Spot. Spot loved to jog every day. One day, while jogging, he found a mint leaf on the ground. Spot thought it smelled nice. Spot picked up the mint leaf and jogged to his friend, Cat. "Look what I found!" said Spot. "It smells good!" Cat sniffed the mint leaf and liked it too. They decided to share the mint leaf. As they sat under a tree, Spot and Cat enjoyed the mint leaf together. They felt happy and loved. They learned that sharing is fun and makes friends happy. From that day on, Spot and Cat shared all their fun things with each other.
Ben was miserable. He had a big ouch on his knee from falling down. He could not go out and play with his friends. He had to stay in his room and rest. He felt sad and lonely. His mom came in with a candle. She said it was a special candle. She said it could send wishes to people who were far away. She said Ben could make a wish and blow out the candle. She said his wish would go to someone who needed it. Ben thought hard. He did not know who to send his wish to. He thought of his grandma, who lived in another town. He missed her a lot. He wished she could come and visit him. He wished she could hug him and read him a story. He wished he could make her happy. He blew out the candle and hoped his wish would come true. He felt a little better. He smiled at his mom and thanked her for the candle. He said he loved her. The next day, Ben got a surprise. His grandma came to see him. She said she had a dream about him. She said she felt his wish in her heart. She said she wanted to come and cheer him up. She hugged him and read him a story. She said she loved him. Ben was happy. He felt his ouch was not so bad. He felt his wish had worked. He felt he had sent some happiness to his grandma. He felt he had a special candle. He felt he was not miserable anymore.
Once upon a time, there were two best friends, Zoe and Louis. Every day, they would go exploring in the forest to find unusual things. One day, as they were walking around, they saw a bright diamond. They were so excited to see it and rushed to pick it up. As soon as they touched it, the diamond started to shine brighter and brighter until it became very hot. Terrified, they threw it away and ran away from it. Suddenly, an old man appears from behind a tree. He said, “Don’t worry! This diamond can serve a special purpose. It can help us find something very unusual!” The two friends were amazed! They asked how they could use the diamond and the old man told them it was simple. They just had to hold it up in the air and spin in circles. The two friends followed the instructions and, sure enough, they soon found a hidden cave. Louis and Zoe couldn't wait to find out what was inside! The old man smiled and said, “This diamond has served its purpose! It has helped you to find something very unusual”. The two friends thanked the old man and, with the diamond in hand, they set off to explore the cave together.
On a bright Wednesday, two friends, Alex and Sarah, decided to go on an adventure. The sun was shining, the sky was blue, and the birds were singing. Alex thought the day was brilliant. First, the two friends went for a walk in the park. They laughed and talked their way through the trees and grass. They stopped to watch the ducks, who splashed in the pond. Then, Alex and Sarah went to the playground. Alex wanted to ride the swings, and Sarah wanted to go down the slide. They both had lots of fun playing on the playground. When they were finished, they stayed in the park to have lunch. The sun was still shining and it was a wonderful afternoon. As they ate, the friends talked and laughed. Finally, it was time to end the adventure. Both Alex and Sarah said goodbye and agreed that their Wednesday had been brilliant fun.

The owl taught Zara about the jungle, the animals, and the plants. Zara listened and learned. She became smarter and smarter. Her friends were happy for her. Now, Zara was not only happy, but smart too. And they all lived happily ever after.

Max's mom saw how dirty he was and said, "Max, you need to wash!" Max did not like to wash, but he knew his mom was right. So, Max went to the big tub to get clean. Max's mom helped him wash all the mud off. She scrubbed his fur, and soon Max was all clean again. Max felt so fresh and happy. He promised his mom that he would try not to get so dirty next time.
Once upon a time, there was a little girl named Lily. Lily lived in a small house with her mom and her dog, Max. One day, Lily and Max went to the park to play with their friends. At the park, Lily saw her friend Tom eating something sour. "What do you have, Tom?" she asked. Tom said, "I have sour candy. Do you want some?" Lily tried the candy and made a funny face. "It's very sour!" she said. Tom laughed and they played together all day. Later, Lily and Max went back to their house. Lily's mom asked, "Did you have fun at the park?" Lily said, "Yes, I had fun with Tom and shared his sour candy." Mom smiled and said, "That's nice, Lily. Sharing with friends is a good thing to do." Lily learned that sharing made her and her friends happy.
Once upon a time, in a bright, colorful world, there was a playful bunny named Bob. Bob loved to hop and play with his friends under the big, beautiful rainbow that always shone in the sky. One day, while Bob was hopping around, he met a new friend named Sally the squirrel. Sally said, "Hi, Bob! Let's play under the rainbow!" So, they went to the rainbow and played all day long. But the next day, when Bob and Sally went to play under the rainbow, it was gone! They looked up and saw a big, gray cloud instead. Bob and Sally were very sad. They wanted to replace the rainbow. Then, a wise old owl named Oliver flew down and said, "Don't worry, my friends. The rainbow will come back after the rain." Bob and Sally didn't know that the rainbow could go away and come back. So, they waited for the rain to stop. And just like Oliver said, the beautiful rainbow came back, bigger and brighter than ever. Bob and Sally were so happy, and they played under the rainbow every day.
Once upon a time there was a silly little bear. He was walking through the forest when he heard a loud noise. He got so scared! He started to run and run, not knowing where to go. He stumbled across a small clearing and saw a big, dark tree. He wanted to climb it and hide. But just then he felt an itch on his paw! He was confused, so he stopped and looked around. Suddenly, a big owl swooped down from the tree above and scared the bear once again! He quickly raced away and ran all the way home. He never came back to the dark tree again, even though he had an itch all over his paw for days. The silly little bear never forgot how scared he was in the forest that day.
Once upon a time, there was a little dog named Spot. Spot was a soft and fluffy dog. One day, Spot heard a loud noise outside. The noise made Spot tremble with fear. He did not know what the noise was, so he hid under the bed. Spot's friend, a little girl named Lily, came into the room. She saw Spot hiding under the bed. "Spot, why are you hiding?" she asked. Spot looked at Lily with his big, round eyes and said, "I heard a loud noise and it scared me." Lily smiled at Spot and said, "Don't worry, Spot. I will go see what the noise is." Lily went outside and saw a big truck. The truck was making the loud noise. Lily came back and told Spot, "It's just a big truck making the noise. You don't have to be scared." Spot came out from under the bed and wagged his tail. He was happy that Lily was there to help him. Now, Spot and Lily could play together without being scared of the noise. And they lived happily ever after.
Once upon a time, in a big zoo, there was a zebra named Ziggy. Ziggy had many friends, like the tall giraffe and the big elephant. They played together every day. One day, the sun was very hot and harsh. Ziggy and his friends were thirsty. They wanted to find water to drink. So, they went on a walk to look for water. On their way, they found a long stick. Ziggy had an idea. Ziggy said, "Let's use the stick to measure how deep the water is when we find it." They all agreed. They walked and walked until they found a small pond. Ziggy used the stick to measure the water. It was not too deep, so they could all drink safely. They drank the water and felt happy. Ziggy and his friends went back home, and they played together until the sun went down.

He picked the biggest and the best one. Bob was very happy. On his way back home, Bob met his friend Tim, a small mouse. They talked and played together. "I have a carrot for Sue," Bob said. "That's nice," said Tim. But then, Bob forgot the carrot at the park when they were done playing. When Bob got home, he looked for the carrot but could not find it. He felt sad. Then, Tim came to his house with the carrot. "You forgot this at the park," said Tim. Bob was so happy and thanked Tim. They went to Sue's house and gave her the carrot. Sue was happy too. The moral of the story is always to help your friends and not to forget things that are important.

Lila and Tom are friends. They like to play near the river. The river is big and blue and makes a nice sound. Sometimes they see fish and ducks in the water. One day, Lila and Tom find a big stick. They pretend it is a boat and they put it in the river. They watch the stick float away. They run along the bank to follow it. "Look, our boat is so fast!" Lila says. "Yes, it is!" Tom says. "Maybe it will go to the sea!" They laugh and clap their hands. They are happy and playful. But then, they see a bridge. The stick goes under the bridge and they can't see it anymore. "Oh no, where is our boat?" Lila says. "I don't know, maybe it is gone!" Tom says. They feel sad and stop running. They look at the bridge and the river. "Maybe we should go back," Lila says. "Yes, maybe we should," Tom says. They turn around and walk back to where they started. They hope their stick will come back to them someday. They leave the river and go home.
Once upon a time, there was a little boy named Tim. He loved to play with his toy radio. He was very enthusiastic about it. He would carry it everywhere he went. The radio played fun songs that made Tim dance and laugh. One sunny day, Tim went to play with his friend, Sue. Sue saw the radio and wanted to play with it too. She asked Tim, "Can I play with your radio, please?" Tim thought about it for a moment and then said, "Okay, but only if we play together." Tim and Sue played with the radio all day long. They danced and sang to the fun songs. They were so happy. But then, it was time for Tim to go home. Sue didn't want to give the radio back. Tim said, "Sue, please surrender my radio. I need to take it home." Sue knew it was the right thing to do, so she gave it back to Tim. In the end, Tim and Sue learned that sharing is caring. They promised to play with the radio together again soon. And they lived happily ever after.
Once upon a time, there was a chubby monkey named Momo. Momo loved to climb trees in the big forest. One day, Momo met a new friend, a small bird named Tweet. Tweet said, "Momo, I can't climb trees like you. Can you help me?" Momo thought for a moment and said, "Yes, Tweet! I will help you climb the tree." Momo had a plan. He found a long vine and tied it to a high branch. He told Tweet, "Hold on to the vine, and I will pull you up." As Momo began to pull the vine, something unexpected happened. Tweet was not going up, but Momo was going down! The chubby monkey was too heavy for the little bird. They both laughed and said, "We can't climb the tree together, but we can still play on the ground." From that day on, Momo and Tweet played together on the ground, and they were the best of friends. And sometimes, Momo would still climb the trees, but he always came back down to play with his friend Tweet.

Tim took the wallet home and hid it under his bed. That night, he had a dream. In the dream, a big scary monster was looking for the wallet. The monster said, "If you don't behave and give the wallet back, I will find you!" Tim woke up scared. The next day, Tim went back to where he found the wallet. He saw a sad man looking for something. Tim knew it was time to behave. He gave the wallet back to the man. The man was so happy and thanked Tim. Tim felt good because he did the right thing.

Once upon a time there was a boy named Jack. He was three years old and loved exploring. One day Jack found a screw on the ground. He picked it up and looked at it. It was a shiny screw with an interesting shape. Jack paused for a moment, not sure what to do with it. He wanted to keep it as a treasure but he was afraid of getting hurt. Finally, he decided to keep it. He said to himself "This will be my special thing". Jack took the screw home and kept it in a safe place. But one day, he thought it would be fun to play with it. Without thinking, he put it in the wall and started turning it. Suddenly, the screw got stuck! Jack tried to remove it but he couldn't. He felt bitter because he had made a mistake. The next morning Jack's dad looked at the wall and saw the screw. He became angry at Jack and yelled "You shouldn't play with these things!" Jack apologized but it was too late. He had to pay a lot of money to repair the wall. Jack learned a lesson that day. He was very sad and bitter and he never touched a screw again.
Once upon a time, there was a wise old cat. The cat liked to play with junk. One day, the cat found a big box of junk. The cat was very happy. The wise cat played with the junk all day. But then, something unexpected happened. The cat found a magic toy in the junk! The magic toy could talk. The cat was very surprised. The magic toy told the cat not to play with junk anymore. The cat felt regret. The cat said sorry to the toy. From that day on, the wise cat never played with junk again. And they lived happily ever after.
Moby was walking through the park with his mom one day. Suddenly, he saw an object that he wanted. He didn't know what it was, but he wanted it. He asked his mom for it and she said no. This made Moby very upset because he really wanted the object. He kept asking for it and his mom kept saying no. Moby started to cry and he got angry. He didn't understand why he couldn't have the object and it made him very upset. Finally, his mom said yes. She said he could have the object, but that he had to be careful with it. Moby was so excited and he hugged his mom. He was happy to finally know that he could have the object. From then on, he always made sure to take good care of it.
One day, a little girl named Mia was playing in her room. She had many toys, but her favorite was a soft, blue bunny. Mia loved to hug her bunny and take it with her everywhere. While playing, Mia saw something appear under her bed. It was a small, green frog! The frog had a hurt head. Mia asked the frog, "Why is your head hurt?" The frog said, "I hit my head on a rock when I jumped too high." Mia wanted to help the frog feel better. She took her blue bunny and put it on the frog's head. The frog smiled and said, "Thank you, Mia. My head feels better now." Mia and the frog became good friends and played together all day.
Once upon a time, there was a big race in the town. All the cars were excited to go fast and win. One car, named Tim, was very careless. He didn't take care of his wheels like the other cars did. On the day of the race, Tim's wheel broke because he was so careless. He could not race with the other cars. Tim was sad and didn't know what to do. Then, a nice man came to help Tim. The nice man helped to repair Tim's wheel. He showed Tim how to take care of his wheels, so they wouldn't break again. Tim was very happy and thanked the nice man. Now, Tim knew how to be careful and was ready to race with the other cars.
Once upon a time, there was a little baby who lived in a crib. The crib was in a small room with lots of toys. The baby had an incredible toy named Mr. Bear. Mr. Bear could talk and play with the baby. One day, the baby's ball rolled under the crib. The baby could not reach it and started to cry. Mr. Bear said, "Don't worry, I will save your ball!" Mr. Bear went under the crib and pushed the ball out with his nose. The baby was happy and stopped crying. The baby said, "Thank you, Mr. Bear! You are incredible!" They played with the ball and had lots of fun. From that day on, the baby and Mr. Bear became the best of friends. They played together every day and had many more incredible adventures.
One day, an enthusiastic otter named Ollie went to the river to play. Ollie loved to spin in the water. He would spin and spin until he got dizzy. While spinning, Ollie saw a big fish. "Hello, big fish!" said Ollie. "Do you want to spin with me?" The big fish looked at Ollie and said, "Yes, I would like to spin with you, Ollie!" They started to spin together. Ollie and the big fish were having so much fun. They spun faster and faster. But then, something unexpected happened. The big fish got too close to Ollie and accidentally bumped him. Ollie felt sad and hurt. He swam away from the big fish and did not want to spin anymore. The big fish felt bad too. Ollie learned to be more careful when spinning with friends.

One day, a little girl named Lily was playing near the diamond. She saw a tiny bug trying to come close to the diamond. Lily was curious and watched the bug. The bug slowly came closer and closer to the diamond. When the bug finally reached the diamond, something magical happened! The important diamond started to glow, and beautiful colors filled the sky. The people in the village were so happy and thanked the little bug. From that day on, the village was a brighter and happier place, all because the little bug came to visit the important diamond.
